### 第37条｜Layout】

A character design reference sheet. **Vertical 9:16 aspect ratio. Maximum resolution output.** Pure solid neutral light-warm-grey studio backdrop. Single-sided side-rim backlighting from camera-right at 45 degrees behind the subject, no fill light, no front light, no other light sources—dramatic cinematic studio lookbook lighting.
 
【Layout】
 
**2x2 grid with unequal panel heights**:
- Top row: two smaller panels (each approximately 1/3 of total canvas height) — head/face close-ups
- Bottom row: two larger panels (each approximately 2/3 of total canvas height) — full body shots
- Thin neutral light-grey dividing lines between all four panels
- All four panels share the same solid backdrop and same lighting setup
 
【4 Panels】
 
**Top-left panel — FRONT FACE CLOSE-UP**:
Tight close-up of the female lieutenant's face, framed from approximately collarbone-line up to top of head. Subject facing camera directly. Side-rim backlight catches the right edge of the face/hair (camera's left side of frame), leaving the left side of the face in deeper shadow with subtle ambient bounce. Both eyes intact and visible, sharp piercing veteran gaze toward camera. Face fully visible—no headwrap covering nose or mouth, headcloth pulled down loose around shoulders/neck.
 
**Top-right panel — SIDE FACE CLOSE-UP (PROFILE)**:
Tight close-up of the lieutenant's face in pure side profile, framed from collarbone-line up to top of head. Subject facing camera-right (so the right side of her face faces camera-right and the LEFT side of her face faces camera). Side-rim backlight catches the back of the head and silhouettes the profile against the backdrop. Long black braided hair visible falling down the back/side, tribal hair ornaments (small bones, beads, metal fragments woven in) catch the rim light. Face fully visible.
 
**Bottom-left panel — FULL BODY FROM NECK DOWN, FRONT VIEW (NO FACE)**:
Full body shot from collarbone-line down to feet, facing camera directly. **The face/head is intentionally cropped out of frame at the top of this panel—only the neck, shoulders, torso, arms, hips, legs, and feet are visible.** This panel is dedicated to body proportions, costume, equipment, weapons, and the mechanical LEFT shin/calf prosthetic—zero face involvement so this panel survives any future face replacement edit. Side-rim backlight catches the right shoulder and right side of body, defining athletic lean silhouette. Dual curved sand-blades held one in each hand or sheathed at hips, all front-side accessories visible.
 
**Bottom-right panel — FULL BODY BACK VIEW (INCLUDING BACK OF HEAD)**:
Full body shot from head to feet, facing directly away from camera (back of subject toward camera). **Includes back of head, headwrap wrapping around skull, long black braid falling down the back to mid-back length.** Short composite recurve bow + small arrow quiver strapped diagonally across back fully visible. Trophy decorations on back of belt visible (more refined and woven than the chieftain's—small braided strands, beads, polished bone fragments). Side-rim backlight catches right shoulder silhouette from this back angle.
 
【Subject — same character across all 4 panels】
 
A female desert raider lieutenant—the chieftain's second-in-command and most lethal close-combat warrior of the Sand Hunter tribe. Same tribal aesthetic family as the chieftain (weathered war-torn ancient lost-tech) but differentiated for individual identity.
 
**Character Core**:
Approximately 30-40 years old, seasoned combat veteran. **Lean wiry athletic combat physique—sinewy and agile, NOT bulky muscular, NOT slender model-fashion thin, NOT voluptuous. Built like an experienced desert raider who survives by speed and precision.**
 
**ETHNICITY — CRITICAL**: Central Asian / Turkic / Sogdian Silk Road ethnic features—the historically accurate "杂胡" mixed-tribe peoples of the Sui-Tang frontier desert (突厥+粟特+西域本土+汉流亡者混合). Specific features:
- **Wide prominent cheekbones** (Central Asian Turkic structure)
- **Deep-set almond-shaped eyes** with monolid or low-fold (NOT round Western double-fold eyes)
- **Black or very dark brown eyes** (NOT blue, NOT green, NOT grey, NOT hazel)
- **Black hair** straight or slightly wavy texture (NOT blonde, NOT red, NOT light brown)
- **Weathered tan-bronze skin** from desert sun (warm bronze undertone, NOT pale Northern European fair, NOT dark African)
- **Strong jawline with full but defined cheek-cheekbone structure** (NOT delicate Western pointed chin, NOT soft round modern East Asian)
- **Straight or slightly aquiline nose** with moderate bridge (NOT thin pointed Western Caucasian nose, NOT flat-bridged modern East Asian nose—more of a Sogdian merchant Silk Road profile)
- **Thick natural eyebrows**, weathered skin texture with sun lines
 
**Battle scars on face**: small diagonal scar through right eyebrow + a thin healed scar on left jawline (these add veteran toughness without disfiguring the features—both eyes fully intact and functional).
 
**Hair**: long black hair gathered into a single thick braid down the back to mid-back length, tribal hair ornaments woven in (small bones, polished beads, small metal rings, small leather wraps). Hair partially tied back to keep face clear, with a few loose strands at temples.
 
**Headwear**: tribal headwrap of weathered tan-and-rust desert cloth, partially wrapped around the head and back of neck but FACE FULLY UNCOVERED in face close-up panels. Wrap can extend down as a hood/scarf around shoulders.
 
**Upper body**: weathered dark leather sleeveless vest cut short above the navel (functional desert combat cut, NOT decorative), exposing toned midriff and arms with battle scars and faded tribal tattoo markings on shoulders, upper arms, and ribs.
 
**Lower body**: form-fitting weathered dark leather trousers or cropped breeches with knee-high desert boots, multiple belt straps and small pouches around waist (more numerous and smaller than the chieftain's—she carries more variety of small tools and ammunition).
 
**Left shin/calf — local mechanical modification**: the LEFT shin and lower leg from below the knee to the ankle replaced with a CRUDE PRIMITIVE BRONZE MECHANICAL PROSTHETIC—same patina aesthetic as the chieftain's right forearm and the wider Sand Hunter tribe's combat exoskeletons. War-torn brass-and-leather construction with visible bolts, leather strap bindings to upper leg stump, exposed gear mechanisms at the knee and ankle, slight verdigris patina (Mecha Crimson Bronze #7A6845, Aged Gold #846035 accent). The mechanical foot ends in a clawed/toed combat-boot-like structure. NOT precision cybernetic, NOT modern, NOT clean—desert blacksmith salvage repair aesthetic.
 
**Trophy decorations**: more refined than the chieftain's brutalist trophies—she wears woven strands of small polished bones, colored thread bindings, small beads, fragments of defeated enemies' jewelry/buckles. Trophies on belt, shoulder strap, and braided into hair.
 
**Footwear**: rugged desert leather boot on right foot wrapped with cloth bindings around the calf (matching the chieftain's style); the left foot is the bronze mechanical prosthetic itself.
 
【Weapons - visible in body panels】
- **Primary**: dual curved scimitar-style sand-blades—two matched single-handed curved blades, sand-pitted iron, bone-and-leather wrapped handles, designed for fast dual-wielding close combat. One held in each hand or sheathed crossed at the back hips. Distinctly NOT identical to the chieftain's massive heavy two-handed war blade—hers are agile, fast, paired.
- **Secondary**: belt of 5-6 small dart-style throwing blades or poisoned darts (smaller and more numerous than the chieftain's throwing knives), small pouch of additional projectiles
- **Ranged**: short composite recurve bow (smaller than the chieftain's full-size bow, designed for quick draw) strapped diagonally across back, small arrow quiver attached
 
【Color Palette】
Desert sand tan, deep burnt umber leather, aged bronze patina (Mecha Crimson Bronze #7A6845 + Aged Gold #846035), dried-blood-red tribal accent on cloth bindings, accent of dark turquoise stone bead detailing in jewelry/hair (Silk Road trade material). ALL materials weathered war-torn dust-coated, NOT clean NOT polished.
 
【Pose - same across all 4 panels】
Default standing pose, agile ready stance—weight balanced on both feet, body slightly turned in athletic readiness, hands resting on or near weapon hilts at hips. Subtle predatory alertness/calm authority—NOT aggressive action pose, NOT relaxed civilian, NOT seductive/sensual pose, NOT fashion-model pose. The body language reads as "an experienced lethal warrior at quiet rest."
 
【Critical consistency】
SAME individual character in all 4 panels—identical face structure (Central Asian/Turkic/Sogdian features), identical scars (right eyebrow + left jawline), identical hair (single thick black braid + tribal ornaments), identical clothing items in identical positions, identical weapons in identical mounts, identical mechanical LEFT shin prosthetic, identical trophy decorations. Same studio backdrop, same single-sided side-rim backlighting setup across all 4 panels.
 
【Lighting - critical】
**Single-sided side-rim backlighting from camera-right at 45-degrees behind subject. NO fill light. NO front light. NO ambient. NO other light sources.** Strong dramatic edge-light catches the right side of subject silhouette in every panel, leaving the left side of subject in deep falloff shadow with subtle ambient bounce. Cinematic high-end fashion-lookbook studio photography (Peter Lindbergh / Annie Leibovitz portrait style with single rim source). Background remains evenly lit solid neutral light-warm-grey backdrop.
 
【Style】
Hyperrealistic photographic character design rendering—shot like a high-end practical costume photographed in a film production studio for a cinematic lookbook reference board. NOT illustration, NOT obvious 3D CGI render. 
 
**Reference aesthetics for Central Asian Silk Road ethnic accuracy**:
- **《长安十二时辰》(The Longest Day in Chang'an, 2019) 西域狼卫** — Sui-Tang Silk Road frontier desert peoples, the most accurate ethnographic anchor for this character's facial features and tribal aesthetic
- **Mongol: The Rise of Genghis Khan (2007)** — Central Asian battle-hardened warrior physique and weathered features (Khulan Chuluun's portrayal of the warrior queen Khulan as facial reference)
- **Monster Hunter Wilds desert hunter aesthetic** — for the tribal armor/weathered-leather/bone-trophy DNA
 
Light Kodak Vision3 35mm film grain texture overlay.
 
【Quality】
Maximum resolution output. 8k ultra clean rendering. Sharp clean edges. Every leather strap, every scar, every bone trophy, every mechanical bolt on the prosthetic clearly defined. Real physical costume cinematic photography aesthetic.
 
【Panel Labels】
Small clean monospace sans-serif black text below each panel:
- Top-left: "FRONT FACE"
- Top-right: "SIDE FACE"
- Bottom-left: "BODY FRONT (NO FACE)"
- Bottom-right: "BACK"
 
Title at top center of canvas (small clean black sans-serif): "SAND HUNTER LIEUTENANT — DEFAULT FORM CHARACTER SHEET"
 
【Constraints】
**Ethnic accuracy (CRITICAL)**: NOT pure Caucasian European face, NOT blonde or red or light brown hair, NOT blue or green or grey or hazel eyes, NOT pale fair Northern European skin, NOT thin pointed delicate Western nose, NOT round Western double-fold eyes, NOT pure Han Chinese face, NOT modern K-pop or Chinese drama actress aesthetic, NOT cute kawaii anime face. MUST be Central Asian Turkic/Sogdian Silk Road mixed ethnicity with appropriate weathered desert-warrior features.
 
**Body & costume**: NOT bulky muscular (lean athletic agile only), NOT slender model-fashion thin, NOT voluptuous sensualized body, NOT seductive pose or framing, NOT young teen-aged appearance (must be 30-40 weathered veteran).
 
**Aesthetic**: NOT precision cybernetic prosthetic (primitive crude war-torn bronze only), NOT medieval European armor, NOT modern military uniform, NOT Ming-Qing imperial costume, NOT samurai armor, NOT belly dancer or fantasy-game-female-warrior chainmail-bikini aesthetic, NOT clean polished anything, NOT shiny new chrome (weathered patina only), NOT cyberpunk neon, NOT obvious CGI 3D render, NOT cartoon illustrated, NOT anime style, NOT colorful saturated palette (muted desert only).

### 第39条｜Layout: 2 columns x 3 rows grid on age

A hand-drawn pencil sketch storyboard on aged off-white paper, in the style of professional film production storyboards. Total image aspect ratio strictly 21:9.

Layout: 2 columns x 3 rows grid on aged paper background. Top-left and top-right are PANEL 4 and PANEL 5. Middle-left and middle-right are PANEL 6 and PANEL 7. Bottom-left is a shooting plan diagram (top-down bird's eye view of the desert with camera positions and 180-degree axis line marked). Bottom-right is a production notes text block. All storyboard panel cells strictly 16:9 horizontal landscape format with thick hand-drawn black ink frame borders.

Beneath each storyboard panel inner frame is a hand-lettered caption strip showing panel number, lens specification, shot type, and brief SFX/action notes in serif block letters slightly uneven.

The drawing technique is loose graphite pencil with confident architectural perspective and clear cinematic composition. Sketches are deliberately gestural but every spatial relationship and prop detail is clearly readable. Selective red colored pencil arrows and emphasis marks indicate movement direction, force vectors, and key dramatic beats.

LIGHTING NOTE FOR ALL PANELS: late afternoon desert golden hour, low sun key light from upper-left 30 degrees. Dense cross-hatching dominates most surfaces representing the shadow base of the warm desert atmosphere. Selective bright untouched paper zones represent the directional sun-key highlights on metal armor surfaces and rim-lit silhouettes. Strong tonal contrast throughout — deep blacks in shadow zones, bright untouched paper for highlights, NOT evenly lit.

Style references: Wes Anderson production storyboards, traditional Hollywood film storyboard aesthetic, animator's pre-visualization sketches, Mad Max Fury Road action sequence boards.

Drawing rules:
- Pencil graphite on warm off-white aged paper background with slight edge wear and faint smudges suggesting heavy production use
- Black ink for panel borders and hand-lettered captions
- Red colored pencil ONLY for arrows, emphasis marks, camera position triangles, and 180-degree axis line
- Cross-hatching for shading; bright highlight zones use untouched paper
- Hand-lettered captions in serif-style block letters, slightly uneven

DIRECTIONAL AXIS RULE: the protagonist ship continues moving deep into frame (forward vector). The chieftain catches up from behind to the female sand hunter's skimmer, takes her rope, plants himself in the sand and pulls backward against the ship's forward motion. The pulling vector is the REVERSE of the ship's forward vector along the same axis line. Cameras stay on one consistent side of this action axis throughout the sequence to maintain spatial continuity.

Title at top center of entire image, hand-lettered in confident block serif: "WANWU SHENG EP1 — SEG 9B — ROPE HANDOFF, PULL, AND SHIP COLLAPSE"

Below title, hand-lettered subtitle in smaller letters: "18 SECONDS / 4 PANELS / SHOOTING PLAN / PRODUCTION NOTES"

PANEL 4 (top-left cell) — OVER-SHOULDER HANDOFF — CHIEFTAIN TAKES ROPE FROM FEMALE SAND HUNTER:
Scene composition: over-the-shoulder shot from behind the female sand hunter. Foreground (lower-left of frame): the back of the female sand hunter's head and shoulder, her braided dark hair and red tribal ribbon headdress sketched in pencil silhouette, her hand visible in mid-frame still gripping the taut rope that extends out of frame toward the upper-right (toward the off-frame ship). Mid-ground (center-right of frame): the fusion-form chieftain figure has just arrived alongside her skimmer — towering massive bronze armored figure with shoulder spiral horns and glowing cyan chest core, extending one massive armored hand toward the rope in the female hunter's grip. The female hunter is in profile, turning her head to look up at the chieftain — her expression shows recognition and deference (eyes wide, mouth slightly open). The chieftain's face (small human face visible above the armor collar) is set in calm focused expression, looking down at the rope as he reaches for it. Background: the rear of the female hunter's skimmer visible at the lower edges of frame, sand plume from the dragging skimmer, distant silhouette of the protagonist ship's rear visible in the upper-right corner of frame still being dragged into frame.

Camera annotation: red pencil arrow from behind the female hunter's shoulder pointing forward toward the chieftain figure, labeled "OTS / female hunter foreground." Small red pencil arrow on the chieftain's hand indicating his reaching motion toward the rope.

Caption strip below panel: "PANEL 4 — OTS HANDOFF / 50mm prime / hand-held with subtle drift / SFX: chieftain heavy footfall arrival, female hunter sharp gasp, rope creak under shifting hands, drag-friction sand grind continuous"

PANEL 5 (top-right cell) — MEDIUM WIDE FRONT — CHIEFTAIN PULLS WITH FULL BODY POWER:
Scene composition: medium-wide front-quarter framing of the chieftain figure planted in the sand desert ground, pulling on the rope. The chieftain occupies the central two-thirds of the frame from feet to head. His body posture: massive bronze armored legs spread wide in a stable wide-base stance with both feet planted deep into the sand (heels visibly cratered into the ground), entire body leaning backward at a steep angle (about 30 degrees from vertical), both armored arms extended forward gripping the rope with both massive hands, the rope visible extending taut into the upper-frame edge toward the off-frame ship. His face: visible at the top of the chest armor, jaw clenched, single visible eye narrowed in focused exertion, the right eye-patch dark in shadow, NOT shouting NOT roaring just contained heavy strain. The cyan chest core glows brighter than baseline (drawn with bright untouched paper radiating outward more strongly than other panels indicating power surge). The rope extends from his grip toward the upper edge of frame, drawn as a perfectly rigid straight diagonal line under maximum tension, with subtle vibration cross-hatching along its length. Beneath his planted feet: the desert sand surface is fractured outward in a radial starburst pattern of deep cracks fanning out in all directions from his feet, sand particles visibly displaced and lifted in clouds around his ankles. Background: faint sketch of the female hunter's skimmer visible behind him at lower frame edges, sand plume from drag, golden hour sky above.

Camera annotation: red pencil arrow from the chieftain's grip along the rope pointing toward the upper frame edge labeled "tension vector / ROPE TAUT TO SHIP." Red pencil radial burst marks around his planted feet labeled "sand fracture under load." Red pencil emphasis at the cyan core labeled "POWER SURGE."

Caption strip below panel: "PANEL 5 — MED-WIDE FRONT-QUARTER / 35mm prime / hand-held with locked-off-feel impact stabilize / SFX: massive armor mechanism groan under load, rope humming at maximum tension, sand cracking under planted feet, low-frequency power throb from chest core, distant ship leg crashes"

PANEL 6 (middle-left cell) — WIDE SIDE SHOT — SHIP SLOWING DOWN UNDER REVERSE PULL:
Scene composition: wide side-profile shot showing the entire battlefield horizontally. Center of frame: the protagonist ship in side view as a long rectangular silhouette with nine mechanical leg shapes underneath — but three of the legs (positioned mid-ship and rear) are visibly stretched and pulled backward at extreme angles, their forward-stride motion compromised. The other six legs continue their galloping forward motion but their rhythm is visibly broken — some mid-stride and faltering. The overall ship body is tilted slightly backward (rear-heavy posture) showing the deceleration. Three taut ropes drawn as rigid diagonal lines extending from the three stretched legs backward and outward to the lower frame corners where the three sand skimmers are dragged trailing behind. The sand plume erupting from beneath the ship is visibly thinner and more broken/sporadic than in earlier galloping shots — the dust output is decreasing as forward speed drops. Behind the ship at lower-left frame: the chieftain figure visible in middle distance still planted and pulling, his rope visible connecting to one specific leg. The other two skimmers behind him also pulling but their effect was negligible — now amplified by the chieftain's force. Foreground (lower frame edge): a strip of cracked sand desert ground with deep tire-track ruts from the dragging skimmers.

Camera annotation: large red pencil arrow showing the ship's diminishing forward direction (drawn shorter than in earlier panels suggesting reduced velocity). Three red pencil opposing arrows along the three taut ropes showing the pulling tension force pointing rearward. Small red pencil annotation at the chieftain figure: "CHIEFTAIN PULL = DECISIVE."

Caption strip below panel: "PANEL 6 — WIDE SIDE SHIP DECELERATION / 50mm prime / lateral hand-held tracking with reduced speed / SFX: ship leg crashes becoming irregular and slowing, mechanical leg-strain metallic groans, rope tension humming at climax, sand plume reducing"

PANEL 7 (middle-right cell) — MASSIVE SLOW COLLAPSE — SHIP TIPPING FORWARD INTO SAND:
Scene composition: wide three-quarter angle shot capturing the moment the protagonist ship loses balance and begins tipping forward into the desert. The ship occupies the central portion of frame, body tilted forward at a steep angle (about 25 degrees from horizontal) with the bow plunging downward toward the sand surface. Three mechanical legs (the ones held by ropes) have collapsed at their knee joints — their joints buckled with armor segments visibly splayed outward at the bending points, the legs splayed in different awkward kneeling angles. The six remaining legs are mid-collapse, unable to compensate, some still mid-stride with feet not yet grounded. The ship's bow has plowed nose-first into the sand surface, ejecting an enormous plume of sand and dust upward and outward in a massive cross-hatched cloud that fills the lower-third of the frame and obscures much of the lower ship body. Sand displaced by the impact erupts in massive radial bursts from the bow contact point. Behind the ship at left frame edge: the chieftain figure visible still holding the now-slackening rope, his pose shifting from straining-backward to standing-firm as the ship goes down. Camera framing should convey the slow weight and inevitability — the ship is going down as if in a controlled fall, not a crash. Background sky: golden hour upper atmosphere with dense cross-hatching, dust cloud rising into the upper sky from the impact.

Camera annotation: red pencil curved arrow showing the ship's tipping motion arcing forward and downward into the sand, labeled "SLOW COLLAPSE." Red pencil radial burst lines at the bow impact point with sand explosion vectors. Small wavy red pencil lines across the entire panel border to indicate "CAMERA SHAKE — ground tremor seismic-level." Red pencil arrow at the chieftain labeled "stance settling."

Caption strip below panel: "PANEL 7 — WIDE 3/4 ANGLE COLLAPSE / 28mm wide / hand-held with seismic shake then settle / SFX: deafening THOOM of bow impact, massive sustained sand-explosion roar, mechanical leg-collapse buckling clanks, sub-bass earth-rumble, end with eerie wind silence"

SHOOTING PLAN (bottom-left cell) — TOP-DOWN BIRD'S EYE OVERVIEW OF THE 9B SEQUENCE:
Drawn as a clean top-down schematic diagram on the aged paper. Center of cell: a sketched simplified outline of the desert terrain as seen from above. Upper portion of cell: the protagonist ship drawn as a rectangular schematic silhouette with nine small leg-marks underneath, with a red pencil directional arrow showing its forward vector (the arrow drawn shorter than in 9A's shooting plan to indicate slowing) and a curved red pencil arc showing where it tips forward and collapses (with a small starburst symbol at the ship's bow indicating the impact point labeled "COLLAPSE POINT"). Behind and to one side: three small triangular schematic outlines representing the three sand skimmers, each connected by a thin line (rope) to one of the ship's leg-marks. One of the three skimmers (the female hunter's) has a small armored figure-mark next to it representing the chieftain who has joined her position. From the chieftain's mark: a thicker red pencil tension arrow pointing rearward along the rope line indicating the dominant pulling force. Four small red pencil triangles marking the camera positions for each of PANEL 4/5/6/7 (P4 OTS camera positioned behind the female hunter pointing at the chieftain; P5 front-quarter camera positioned in front of the chieftain pointing at his torso; P6 wide side camera positioned perpendicular to the action axis pointing at the ship; P7 wide 3/4 camera positioned at a side-rear angle to the ship pointing at the collapse). A dashed red pencil straight line running across the diagram along the ship's forward vector and the rope tension axis — this is the 180-degree axis line. Labels in hand-lettered serif block letters: "180° AXIS LINE", "P4 / P5 / P6 / P7 CAMERA POSITIONS", "SHIP DECELERATION", "CHIEFTAIN ANCHOR POINT", "COLLAPSE POINT", "FEMALE HUNTER SKIMMER", "OTHER 2 SKIMMERS DRAGGED".

Caption strip below the shooting plan cell: "SHOOTING PLAN — 180° AXIS PRESERVED ACROSS ALL 4 SHOTS / cameras stay on one side of action axis / spatial continuity maintained / collapse sequence covered with progressively wider angles"

PRODUCTION NOTES BLOCK (bottom-right cell) — A hand-lettered text-only cell containing key production memos in confident serif block letters across multiple paragraphs:
Block heading: "PRODUCTION NOTES — SEGMENT 9B"
Paragraph 1: "Continuity: Segment 9A closes with chieftain charging forward in orbit power run. Segment 9B opens with chieftain arriving at female sand hunter's skimmer to take the rope. No establishing shot needed — Panel 4 OTS framing communicates the handoff geometry directly."

Panel 7 is also approximately 6 seconds — the slow collapse must convey enormous weight; rushing the collapse defeats the purpose. Total runtime 18 seconds."
Paragraph 3: "Sound design philosophy: NO musical score. Sound design only — ambient desert wind, mechanical groans, rope tension humming, ship leg crashes becoming irregular and stopping, climactic bow-impact THOOM, end with eerie wind silence after the dust settles. Chieftain has NO dialogue throughout."

Paragraph 5: "Next segment hook: After ship collapses in Panel 7, the dust slowly settles to reveal the new tactical situation — protagonists trapped inside a downed ship surrounded by sand hunters and the chieftain. Segment 10 begins with this reveal."

Hard constraints:

- Total image strictly 21:9 aspect ratio

- Female sand hunter character appearance consistent in PANEL 4
- Protagonist ship appearance consistent across PANEL 6 and PANEL 7 — same nine-leg rectangular silhouette design


### 第40条｜REMOVE these AI-generation artifacts】

Process this image to remove all AI-generation artifacts and restore an authentic live-action cinematic film quality. The composition, subjects, scene elements, and overall color palette must remain 100% identical — only fix the quality artifacts.

【REMOVE these AI-generation artifacts】
- Over-sharpening artifacts, crispy edges, zipper-like jagged seams on high-contrast boundaries
- Digital noise — harsh pixel-level grain (NOT to be confused with film grain)
- Edge halos around figures and objects
- Plastic / waxy / synthetic / over-rendered look on skin and organic surfaces
- Etched / engraved feeling around object edges
- Micro-contrast over-pumping that makes textures feel "drawn" rather than photographed
- The hyper-processed AI-generated quality that gives away the image as synthetic

【APPLY these natural film qualities】
- Natural 35mm film grain — subtle, organic, evenly distributed across the entire image
- Soft photographic texture transitions on all surfaces
- Organic lens-realistic edge softness — like real camera optics, not digital sharpening
- Smooth lighting and shadow transitions, no banding
- Physically realistic specular highlights — soft and rolled off, not blown out
- Subtle atmospheric depth — distant elements slightly softened by air haze
- Live-action film color grading — natural skin tones, balanced saturation

【Per-element treatment】
- Skin: keep visible pores and natural skin texture, but remove the plastic over-rendered look
- Fabric: keep weave and fold structure, but soften over-sharpened threads
- Stone / wood / metal: keep weathering and material grain, but soften crispy AI edges
- Hair: keep individual strand visibility, but remove the "every-strand-engraved" plastic look
- Background / distant elements: add natural atmospheric softness and depth-of-field falloff
- Architecture / large structures: keep all detail but soften the over-rendered geometric crispness

【STRICTLY PRESERVE — do not change】
- 100% identical composition, framing, aspect ratio
- 100% identical subjects — every face, body, clothing, object, environmental element
- 100% identical color palette — do not shift hues, do not re-grade colors
- Identical lighting direction, intensity, and mood
- All scene elements remain exactly where they were

【NEVER do these】
- NEVER add new elements that were not in the original
- NEVER remove elements that were in the original
- NEVER change composition, framing, or subjects' identities
- NEVER apply cartoon, illustrated, painted, or stylized look
- NEVER shift the overall color palette warmer or cooler
- NEVER brighten or darken the image overall
- NEVER add filters, effects, overlays, vignettes, or color tints
- NEVER reduce overall detail — the goal is to soften artifacts, not to lose information

### 第43条｜万物生第1集 沙猎部落单人沙艇 载具设计图 v1

**万物生第1集 沙猎部落单人沙艇 载具设计图 v1**
【格式】
- 16:9横版+最高分辨率
- 4格均等2x2 = SIDE + FRONT + TOP + 3/4 VIEW
- 同样的studio backdrop+single-side rim backlight环境（继承沙猎部落photoshoot DNA）

【沙艇定位】
**单人载具**——每个普通沙匪开自己的一艘，多艘从不同方向围猎+各自钩索拉拽="像捕鲸船一样把江流飞船拽停"的战术逻辑。一人独立完成驾驶+发射钩索+战斗。

【关键设计决策】
- **体积小**：约2.5米长（不是Viking longship+不是多人战舰）
- **视觉参考**：现代陆地帆板（land windsurfer）+ sand sled + 古代独木沙舟
- **单人操作位**：站立或半坐半站
- **混合动力**：小帆（主）+ 简化青铜机改驱动器（辅）
- **滑行机制**：沙橇底+辅助小轮
- **部族DNA**：war-torn salvage青铜机改+和合体外骨骼+人物装备同DNA

A vehicle design reference sheet. **Horizontal 16:9 aspect ratio. Maximum resolution output.** Pure solid neutral light-warm-grey studio backdrop. Single-sided side-rim backlighting from camera-right at 45 degrees behind the subject, no fill light, no front light, no other light sources—dramatic cinematic studio lookbook lighting. Same studio environment as the Sand Hunter character sheets—reads as part of the same photoshoot session.

【Layout】

**2x2 equal grid**:
- Top-left: SIDE VIEW (full broadside profile, the primary view of the vehicle)
- Top-right: FRONT VIEW (head-on, showing prow and frontal silhouette)
- Bottom-left: TOP-DOWN VIEW (god's-eye view from directly above showing footprint and rigger layout)
- Bottom-right: 3/4 PERSPECTIVE VIEW (three-quarter angle showing depth and dimensionality, hero shot)

Thin neutral light-grey dividing lines between all four panels. Vehicle centered in each panel with even space margin.

【Subject — same vehicle across all 4 panels】

A Sand Hunter tribe **single-pilot sand skiff**—a small fast desert hunting vessel designed for one raider to operate alone. Pack hunting tactic: multiple skiffs (5-8 of these) swarm a target from different directions, each pilot launching grappling hooks to anchor the prey from multiple angles like harpoon-boats around a whale.

**Overall form (CRITICAL silhouette and scale)**:
A compact single-person desert vessel approximately **2.5 meters long, 0.8 meters wide, 0.6 meters tall** (not counting mast and sail). Think the silhouette of a **land-windsurfer board crossed with a small dugout canoe crossed with a sand sled**—a slender pointed hull with a single standing/half-seated position for one pilot. The hull tapers to a sharp prow and a slightly upswept stern. NOT a large multi-crew ship. NOT a Viking longship. NOT a Mad Max truck. Single-pilot scale only.

**Hull construction**:
Primary hull made of **weathered dark hardwood** carved/hewn into a single dugout-canoe-style shape (one solid piece with shaped interior cavity), reinforced with **bronze rivets and leather lashings** at stress points. War-torn salvage hand-crafted tribal carpentry, NOT factory-precision, NOT clean modern. Patches of mismatched repair wood show old battle damage. Tribal geometric patterns burned into the upper hull edges with branding-iron-style markings (matching the same runic-glyph aesthetic as the rest of the Sand Hunter tribe's bronze equipment).

**Bottom — sand-sled mechanism**:
The hull rides on **a single central long bronze-shod sand runner/skid** running the full length underneath (think: one large central ski-blade). The bronze runner shows heavy wear, sand-pitting, slight verdigris patina. Flanking the central skid, **2 small outrigger bronze-spoked auxiliary wheels** mounted on simple suspension struts on each side help with directional stability and tight turns.

**Salvaged ancient lost-tech drive — small bronze mechanism mid-hull**:
Mounted in a small recessed compartment in the rear-third of the hull is a **compact salvaged ancient bronze mechanical drive unit**—looted lost-tech, retrofitted with leather strapping and crude iron bolts. Smaller and simpler than a multi-crew ship's drive: just a single bronze gear-cluster with exposed clockwork, slight verdigris patina (Mecha Crimson Bronze #7A6845 + Aged Gold #846035—same DNA as Sand Hunter combat exoskeletons and prosthetics). Provides auxiliary thrust when wind fails.

**Mast and sail**:
A **single short slender mast** rising about 2.5 meters above the deck, hand-hewn wood, dark weathered. **Small triangular lateen-style sail** of weathered tan-and-rust desert canvas, hand-stitched, with tribal geometric patterns dyed in dried-blood-red across the sail face. Smaller and more agile than a longship's square sail—designed for single-pilot rigging and quick maneuvering. Rigging of weathered hemp ropes, small bronze pulleys, leather reinforcement. In the SIDE VIEW the sail is partially furled showing structure; in the 3/4 VIEW the sail is fully unfurled in a dramatic billow.

**Prow decoration — small tribal totem**:
At the upswept prow, a **small tribal totem affixed to the prow tip**: a single curved animal horn (suggestive of a small ram or sand-creature) bound with leather cords, plus 1-2 small bone fragments hanging beneath. NOT a large beast-skull figurehead (that's reserved for chieftain's flagship). Small-scale tribal marking appropriate for a common raider's solo skiff.

**Pilot position (single-person operation)**:
Located in the **forward-middle of the deck**: a **single standing/half-seated pilot position** with:
- A small wooden tiller/steering arm reaching back to the rear (foot-operated or hand-operated)
- A leather-padded standing rest or knee-rest (the pilot stands semi-braced against this during fast runs, can lean back into it)
- A vertical handhold post or small wooden grip attached to the deck for the off-hand
- **A bronze mounting plate just forward of the pilot position** where a portable hook launcher locks in (the pilot can fire the launcher one-handed while steering with the other hand and feet)
- Leather grip straps along the gunwales to keep the pilot braced during high-speed turns and combat impacts
- A small belt-pouch mount near the pilot's hip for spare grappling hooks/ammo

**Trophy decorations on hull**:
SPARSE — only 2-3 small bone fragments and one small fragment of defeated enemy equipment (broken belt buckle or weapon shard) tied with leather cords to the stern or upper hull strake. Markedly less than a chieftain's flagship would carry. This is a working raider's solo vessel, not a trophy display.

【Color Palette】
Hull: deep weathered umber wood with sun-bleached lighter patches. Bronze fittings/runners/drive: Mecha Crimson Bronze #7A6845 + Aged Gold #846035 with verdigris patina. Sail and rigging: weathered tan canvas + dried-blood-red tribal markings. Horn totem: aged ivory-yellow bone. Leather lashings: deep umber. Overall palette: muted desert tones, NOT colorful, NOT saturated.

【4 Panels detail】

**Top-left panel — SIDE VIEW**: Pure broadside profile view. Vehicle facing camera-right (prow to the right). Full length visible from horn-totem prow to stern tiller. Single central bronze runner visible underneath the hull with the small outrigger wheels flanking. Mast vertical with sail partially furled showing sail material and rigging. Side hull tribal carvings, sparse trophy decorations, pilot position with mounting plate all visible. Side-rim backlight catches the upper hull edge and mast/sail.

**Top-right panel — FRONT VIEW**: Vehicle prow facing camera directly. Small horn totem at top center of frame. Hull tapers visibly to the prow point. Single central bronze runner visible underneath with the two outrigger wheels visible flanking on each side. Mast and partial sail visible rising from behind the prow.

**Bottom-left panel — TOP-DOWN VIEW**: God's-eye view from directly overhead looking down at the deck. Shows the slender single-person footprint: mast and sail rigging amidships, single pilot standing position with tiller behind, bronze mounting plate forward of pilot, small drive unit recessed at rear-third. The narrow elongated single-person form factor is clearly readable from above.

**Bottom-right panel — 3/4 PERSPECTIVE VIEW**: Three-quarter angle showing depth, approximately 30-45 degrees from front-side, slightly elevated viewpoint. Both side profile and prow visible. Sail FULLY UNFURLED in this view—dramatic billow showing the small triangular lateen sail size and tribal markings. Single central bronze runner catches the rim light underneath. The "hero shot" of the four views.

【Critical consistency】
SAME individual vehicle in all 4 panels—identical hull dimensions and proportions, identical horn totem at prow, identical sail size and markings, identical drive unit position, identical sparse trophy decoration arrangement, identical bronze runner and outrigger wheel configuration, identical pilot position layout. Same studio backdrop, same single-sided side-rim backlighting setup across all 4 panels.

【Lighting - critical】
**Single-sided side-rim backlighting from camera-right at 45-degrees behind subject. NO fill light. NO front light. NO ambient. NO other light sources.** Strong dramatic edge-light catches the right side of the vehicle silhouette, leaving the left side in deep falloff shadow with subtle ambient bounce. (For TOP-DOWN VIEW the rim-light still comes from "camera right" within that panel's compass.)

【Style】
Hyperrealistic photographic vehicle design rendering—shot like a high-end practical prop vehicle photographed in studio for a film production design board. NOT illustration, NOT obvious 3D CGI render.

**Reference aesthetics for single-pilot desert craft**:
- **Modern land windsurfer / sand sailing board** — for the single-pilot wind-driven desert craft functional logic and slender silhouette
- **Indigenous dugout canoe construction** — for the hand-hewn single-piece wooden hull aesthetic
- **Mad Max Fury Road war boys minor vehicles (the smaller ones, NOT the big rigs)** — for the war-torn salvage scrap-and-bolt construction style and tribal modification aesthetic
- **Studio Ghibli Nausicaä of the Valley of the Wind small tribal vehicles** — for hand-crafted weathered organic-mechanical fusion
- **Monster Hunter Wilds nomadic desert culture** — tribal weathered-leather and bone-fragment DNA (consistent with the Sand Hunter character sheets and other equipment)

Light Kodak Vision3 35mm film grain texture overlay.

【Quality】
Maximum resolution output. 8k ultra clean rendering. Sharp clean edges. Every wood grain on the hull, every bronze rivet, every gear tooth on the drive unit, every leather lashing knot, every tribal carving clearly defined. Real physical practical-prop cinematic photography aesthetic.

【Panel Labels】
Small clean monospace sans-serif black text below each panel:
- Top-left: "SIDE"
- Top-right: "FRONT"
- Bottom-left: "TOP"
- Bottom-right: "3/4 VIEW"

Title at top center of canvas (small clean black sans-serif): "SAND HUNTER SOLO SKIFF — VEHICLE DESIGN SHEET"

【Constraints】

**Scale and crew**: NOT multi-crew ship, NOT large vessel, NOT longship-sized (must be small single-pilot 2.5 meters long), NOT 2-person, NOT 3-person, NOT crewed (single pilot only).

**Form factor**: NOT a water boat (this is a sand-sled with sail, NOT a water vessel), NOT a wheeled cart/buggy (wheels are auxiliary stabilizers, NOT primary motion—runners are primary), NOT a modern desert dune buggy, NOT a motorcycle, NOT a hovercraft, NOT a flying vehicle.

**Aesthetic anchoring**: NOT precision cybernetic, NOT clean modern engineering (war-torn salvage tribal handcraft only), NOT steampunk Victorian aesthetic (this is ancient lost-tech NOT industrial-revolution), NOT shiny new chrome metal (weathered patina only), NOT colorful paint job (muted desert tones only), NOT cyberpunk neon, NOT obvious CGI 3D render, NOT cartoon, NOT anime, NOT fantasy elf-craft elegance.

**Decoration restraint**: NOT large beast-skull figurehead (that's chieftain's flagship—use small horn totem only), NOT heavy quantity of trophy decorations (sparse 2-3 only), NOT ornate carvings or jewelry (tribal geometric burns only).

**Layout**: NOT 1:1 square aspect (must be horizontal 16:9), NOT 9:16 vertical, NOT colorful background (solid neutral light-warm-grey only), NOT soft even neutral lighting (must be single-side rim backlight only), NOT front light, NOT fill light, NOT any text other than the four panel labels and the title.

【实操注意】

1. 跑出来重点检查：
   - **单人载具**（最大风险）——容易跑成多人战舰。必须是2.5米长slender single-pilot scale，不是longship不是Mad Max big rig
   - **沙橇底+辅助轮**——主滑行是runner不是wheels。如果跑成纯轮式车辆+按"runners are primary motion, wheels are stabilizer auxiliary"加强
   - **单central runner + 2 outrigger wheels**配置——具体的滑行机制
   - **小三角lateen帆**——不是longship的大方帆+不是现代windsurfer的钣金帆
   - **小horn totem + sparse trophies**——区别于chieftain flagship会有的大beast-skull和refined decoration
   - **war-torn salvage部族手工感**——不是modern desert buggy+不是steampunk+不是科幻精良
   - **族群DNA一致**：和合体外骨骼+人物装备同青铜patina+#7A6845/#846035色板
   - **16:9横版**+4格均等2x2

2. 跑歪兜底加强:
   - 跑成多人战舰（最大风险）→ "STRICTLY single-pilot vessel, 2.5 meters long, slender single-person craft, NOT a multi-crew ship, NOT longship-sized"
   - 跑成纯轮式车 → "PRIMARY motion is via single central bronze runner/skid underneath, wheels are only small auxiliary stabilizers, NOT a wheeled vehicle"
   - 跑成water boat → "DESERT SAND vehicle riding on top of sand via runner, NOT a water boat, NOT for water use"
   - 跑成现代land windsurfer → "ancient tribal hand-crafted hardwood hull with bronze fittings, NOT modern fiberglass board, NOT modern sport equipment"
   - 跑成steampunk → "ancient lost-tech bronze mechanical drive, NOT Victorian industrial steampunk, NOT brass-plumbing aesthetic"
   - 跑成大beast-skull figurehead → "small horn totem on prow only, NOT large skull figurehead, decorative restraint appropriate for common raider's solo vessel"
   - 跑成精良战舰 → "war-torn salvage tribal handcraft with mismatched repairs, hand-hewn dugout-canoe style hull, NOT factory-precision boat-building"

整段4秒一个连续长镜头+no cuts no transitions+严格single take。

【机位与构图】
机位:close-up特写+相机位于江流后方略上方位置+从江流后脑勺斜上方拍向他的头部顶端+江流的后脑勺与脖子后侧占画面下半部约二分之一+
@图3 萌螃蟹蹲在江流脑袋顶上完整入画占画面上半部约二分之一+背景是甲板上方的天空与船尾建筑结构上沿。

镜头:50mm prime lens标准焦段+rectilinear lens projection直线投影+no barrel distortion+no fisheye+geometric lines straight。

光线:#F5C870金色夕阳从画面侧面低角度斜光打在江流后脑勺与萌螃蟹外壳上+江流棕黑头发右侧被金边镶光左侧#1A1610阴影+萌螃蟹青铜外壳右半部被金光镶边#7A6845主色加#F5C870高光+大气中可见尘土漂浮+atmospheric haze。

【画面内容】
画面下半部:
@图1 江流的后脑勺占据+棕黑过渡色#2A1F18短发可见+发丝有自然纹理+颈部#2D2A28深灰金属机械义体上半部分可见+屏脸面具的侧后方边缘轮廓在画面下边缘可见但完全不显示正脸+江流身体保持站姿前倾的操船状态+双手不入画但能感觉江流站在船舵前。

画面上半部:
@图3 萌螃蟹蹲在江流脑袋顶上+八条小铜腿牢牢扒住江流的棕黑头发+两个#5BB8E0蓝色圆形大眼睛朝向画面右后方(主角船尾方向+刘回所在方位)+萌螃蟹的#7A6845铜青绿外壳与#846035暗金符号刻面在夕阳下泛光+#4FC3D9青色关节缝隙微微发光。

【开篇阶段】
萌螃蟹的两个蓝色大眼睛从注视前方状态+突然瞪大瞳孔放大+随即萌螃蟹整个身体在江流脑袋顶上做一个灵活的360度原地转身+八条小铜腿牢牢扒住江流头发不动+只有萌螃蟹的本体上半身做转动+像一个旋转的瓶盖+转身完成后萌螃蟹的两个蓝色大眼睛瞪向画面右后方观察威胁来源。

【中段+末段阶段】
萌螃蟹立马吓得一蹦跶+整个身体从江流脑袋顶猛地向上弹起一下约5厘米高度+八条小铜腿短暂离开江流头发+萌螃蟹在空中身体微微抖动+然后稳稳落回江流脑袋顶+八条小铜腿重新扒紧+但萌螃蟹接下来不停做小幅上蹿下跳+每秒2-3次的频率+像看到鬼一样恐慌不止+蓝色大眼睛持续圆瞪。

同步:江流上半身在听到萌螃蟹台词中"狼王"二字的瞬间+整个上身微微僵硬+肇肩(后脖子两侧)向上提了约2厘米+后脑勺微微向左偏+表达"他也听到了狼王这个名字+他认识"的反应+但江流没有任何头部转动+只有这个微僵+耸肩+微偏的瞬间反应+然后保持紧绷站姿。

萌螃蟹同时持续天津话快语速尖嗓子毒舌怒气台词:"我滴个亲娘嘞!狼王老张麻子都来啦?!这下完啦!"——参考@音频1 的音色调性。

【声音设计】
开篇:沙艇引擎呼啸的低频drone+9条机械腿远景持续crunchy落沙脚步循环+船舵金属吱呀(从画面外传来)+布幡rip-flap撕扯风声+大漠风声+萌螃蟹蹲扒头发的微小金属clink。

中段+末段:萌螃蟹天津话尖嗓子毒舌怒气快语速台词:"我滴个亲娘嘞!狼王老张麻子都来啦?!这下完啦!"+萌螃蟹声线参考@音频1 +萌螃蟹蹦跳起跳落地的金属clink小响声循环+江流屏脸面具发出微弱电子哔哔嗡鸣回应"狼王"二字时的反应+背景持续沙艇引擎与机械脚步。

全程:仅生成动作音效与对白音效与环境音效与屏脸电子音效+不要bgm+不要抒情背景音乐+sound design only ambient and action sounds NO background music+对白天津话发音+小宠物配音是尖嗓子毒舌怒气小动物声线参考@图5。

【关键约束】
机位铁律:全程close-up特写固定机位+从江流后侧上方斜俯拍向脑袋顶+江流后脑勺占画面下半部+萌螃蟹蹲脑袋顶占画面上半部+无变焦+无dolly+无环绕+只有handheld微抖。

后脑勺铁律:整段视频全程仅显示江流的后脑勺与脖子后侧+绝对不显示江流的正脸+绝对不显示江流的屏脸面具完整正面+绝对不让相机绕到江流前方+江流的头部不做明显转动+只有微僵+肇肩上提+后脑勺微偏的瞬间反应。

头发色铁律:江流的头发是#2A1F18棕黑过渡色+介于深棕与漆黑之间+不是纯黑#1A1610+不是漆黑+发丝在夕阳侧光下能看出棕色调的反光+绝对不能渲染成纯黑头发。

萌螃蟹形态铁律:萌螃蟹全程默认萌螃蟹形态+八条腿+两个圆形蓝色大眼睛+不可变成猴子+不可变成鸟+不可变成人形+不可有手有嘴+灵活的360度转身仅是上半身旋转+八条腿持续扒住江流头发+蹦跳起跳后落回江流脑袋顶+不离开。

江流反应铁律:江流仅在听到"狼王"二字瞬间有上身微僵+肇肩上提+后脑勺微偏的瞬间反应+不做大幅头部转动+不做面部表情戏(后脑勺视角看不到)+其余全程保持操船姿态。

风格铁律:35mm film stock photography texture+analog film grain+photochemical look+NOT 3D rendered+NOT CGI+NOT video game graphics+NOT Unreal Engine render+NOT Blender render+NOT clean digital surface+NOT plastic look+all surfaces must show real physical wear and weathering+film print quality not digital cinema clean.

NOT slow motion+NOT speed ramping+NOT静态镜头+NOT正脸+NOT屏脸正面特写+NOT江流头部大幅转动+NOT萌螃蟹离开江流脑袋+NOT萌螃蟹变形+NOT cyberpunk neon+NOT clear blue sky+NOT cartoon Q版+NOT illustrated+NOT chibi style+NOT exaggerated cute deformation+NOT普通话发音+NOT纯黑头发+无字幕+无水印。


### 第46条｜Layout: 3 columns x 2 rows grid on age

A hand-drawn pencil sketch storyboard on aged off-white paper, in the style of professional film production storyboards. Total image aspect ratio strictly 21:9.

Layout: 3 columns x 2 rows grid on aged paper background. Each of the 6 cells contains one element. Top row (left to right): Panel 1, Panel 2, Panel 3. Bottom row (left to right): Panel 4, Panel 5, Shooting Plan diagram. Each of Panels 1-5 is strictly 16:9 horizontal landscape format with thick hand-drawn black ink frame border. The Shooting Plan cell in the bottom-right is a top-down bird's eye view of the desert battlefield with camera positions and 180-degree axis line marked.

Beneath each storyboard panel inner frame is a hand-lettered caption strip showing panel number, lens specification, shot type, and brief SFX/action notes in serif block letters slightly uneven.

The drawing technique is loose graphite pencil with confident architectural perspective and clear cinematic composition. Sketches are deliberately gestural but every spatial relationship and prop detail is clearly readable. Selective red colored pencil arrows and emphasis marks indicate movement direction, force vectors, and key dramatic beats.

LIGHTING NOTE FOR ALL PANELS: late afternoon desert golden hour, low sun key light from upper-left 30 degrees. Dense cross-hatching dominates most surfaces representing the shadow base of the warm desert atmosphere. Selective bright untouched paper zones represent the directional sun-key highlights on metal armor surfaces and rim-lit silhouettes. Strong tonal contrast throughout — deep blacks in shadow zones, bright untouched paper for highlights, NOT evenly lit.

Style references: Wes Anderson production storyboards, traditional Hollywood film storyboard aesthetic, animator's pre-visualization sketches, Mad Max Fury Road action sequence boards, Spider-Man train-stop sequence storyboards.

Drawing rules:
- Pencil graphite on warm off-white aged paper background with slight edge wear and faint smudges suggesting heavy production use
- Black ink for panel borders and hand-lettered captions
- Red colored pencil ONLY for arrows, emphasis marks, camera position triangles, force vectors, and 180-degree axis line
- Cross-hatching for shading; bright highlight zones use untouched paper
- Hand-lettered captions in serif-style block letters, slightly uneven

DIRECTIONAL AXIS RULE: the protagonist ship is moving deep into the battlefield (forward vector toward one consistent direction). Liu Hui stands as the anchor at the rope's near end, pulled toward the ship's direction by rope tension. Two remaining sand skimmers are dragged behind the ship between Liu Hui and the ship — connected to the ship's mechanical legs by their own ropes. The female hunter's third skimmer has been knocked off the chain in the prior segment — it now drifts off-axis on one side, separated from the main battlefield. The action axis runs from Liu Hui through the ship; cameras stay on one consistent side of this axis throughout the sequence to maintain spatial continuity.

Title at top center of entire image, hand-lettered in confident block serif: "WANWU SHENG EP1 — SEG 9B — THE SHIP COMES DOWN"

Below title, hand-lettered subtitle in smaller letters: "MULTI-SHOT HARD CUT / 3 SHOTS / 5 STORYBOARD PANELS + SHOOTING PLAN / SCALE CONTRAST CLIMAX"

PANEL 1 (top-left cell) — SHOT 1: EXTREME CLOSE-UP OF THE HAND GRIPPING THE ROPE:
Scene composition: extreme close-up framing dominated by Liu Hui's massive bronze mechanical hand gripping the rope at the center of frame. The hand occupies roughly 60% of the frame area — every detail of the armor plating, weathered patina, etched ancient gold symbols, and the tension in the knuckles is rendered with confident pencil shading. The rope passes through the hand horizontally across the frame, stretched taut and straight — visible fibers in the rope show micro-trembling under extreme tension (rendered as short fine pencil lines along the rope's length). The lower-left portion of frame shows the bottom half of Liu Hui's face — chin and lower jaw to the bridge of the nose visible — jaw muscles clenched, teeth gritted, lips pressed tight. The face is partially in shadow with cross-hatching, the highlighted edge of the jaw rim-lit by the golden hour sun from upper-left. Background behind the hand and face: completely defocused with light pencil hatching suggesting motion blur of the desert flowing past — the entire frame conveys that everything is still in motion despite the close-up framing.

Camera annotation: red pencil emphasis marks on the trembling rope fibers, labeled "rope fibers micro-tremble." Red pencil indicator inside the panel showing camera handheld micro-shake.

Caption strip below panel: "SHOT 1 / PANEL 1 — EXTREME CLOSE-UP HAND ON ROPE / 85mm prime / handheld with micro-shake, no large camera movement / SFX: rope tensioning humming building rapidly, low contained throat groan from Liu Hui, jaw-clenching mechanical creak"

**HARD CUT.**

PANEL 2 (top-middle cell) — SHOT 2: SIDE 90° WIDE — THE SCALE CONTRAST CLIMAX:
Scene composition: this is the dramatic anchor of the entire storyboard — the camera is at a wide side 90° angle to the action axis. Liu Hui occupies the lower-left quadrant of the frame in his anchored-resistance pose, captured in full side profile: both arms extended forward gripping the rope, both heels dragging in the sand creating two visible deep parallel furrows that extend from beyond the lower frame edge into the middle distance, body leaning backward at a slight angle resisting the rope's pull, head lowered, jaw clenched, the cyan core at chest and shoulders glowing strongly. Liu Hui is rendered at small-to-medium scale relative to the frame — his height fills approximately the lower 1/4 to 1/5 of frame height.

The protagonist ship occupies the right two-thirds of the frame in the middle-to-deep distance — drawn with its complete rectangular bronze-mechanized side profile visible, the wooden deck on top, and all 9 mechanical legs underneath in clear pencil detail. The ship's overall height in frame is approximately 4 to 5 times the height of Liu Hui — this scale contrast is the central dramatic anchor of the entire storyboard. Three of the ship's 9 mechanical legs (the ones strained by the rope tension) are visibly pulled back at extreme angles toward the lower-left of frame, with armor segments visibly deformed under stress. The remaining 6 legs are still galloping forward (toward the upper-right of frame, deeper into the distance) but in a faltering broken rhythm — some legs caught mid-stride awkwardly, others planted at off-balance angles. The ship's body is tilted slightly backward overall, showing the deceleration moment. The ship's stern sand-dust plume is reduced — thinner and more broken than in earlier segments.

Between Liu Hui and the ship in the middle distance: two smaller sand skimmers, each connected by their own rope to one of the ship's legs, being dragged in the chain — both visibly struggling in the deceleration. Far right edge of frame, off-axis from the main chain: the female hunter's third skimmer drifting off to the side at a clearly different angle from the main action, separated from the chain — drawn smaller and isolated. The rope from Liu Hui's hands extends in a single taut diagonal line across the entire frame from lower-left to upper-right, connecting to one of the ship's strained mechanical legs.

Background sky: dense cross-hatching for golden hour atmosphere, the entire scene rim-lit from the upper-left golden sun creating strong silhouetting on the ship.

Camera annotation: red pencil arrow showing camera position at side 90° angle to the action axis, labeled "side wide / 90° / scale anchor." Red pencil force-vector arrows along the rope showing tension force between Liu Hui and the ship. A red pencil scale-reference bracket on the left edge of frame indicating Liu Hui's height vs the ship's height — clearly showing the 1:4 to 1:5 ratio.

Caption strip below panel: "SHOT 2 / PANEL 2 — SIDE 90° WIDE — SCALE CONTRAST / 24mm wide / lateral tracking + handheld / SFX: ship's 9-leg crashes becoming irregular and slowing, mechanical leg-strain metallic groans, rope humming at climax, background desert wind"

**HARD CUT.**

PANEL 3 (top-right cell) — SHOT 3 OPENING: THE SHIP STARTS TO LOSE BALANCE:
Scene composition: camera at approximately 3/4 angle behind Liu Hui, looking past him into the deeper space where the ship is. Liu Hui occupies the foreground left area as a partial silhouette (back, shoulders, the bronze spiral horns rising into the upper frame, the cyan core glowing softly through the silhouette from the front side) — he is still in the anchored pose but the rope tension is at its peak, his body bracing. The protagonist ship dominates the middle and right area of frame — it is in the moment of beginning to lose balance: the ship's body is now tilted forward at a noticeable angle (front of ship dipping toward the sand), three of the mechanical legs (the rope-strained ones) are visibly buckling at the knee joints with the buckling just beginning, the other 6 legs are caught in mid-collapse motion — some legs unable to step properly, some planted at clearly off-balance angles. The ship is in motion but the forward motion is collapsing into downward motion. The two remaining sand skimmers in the middle distance are visibly thrown off-balance by the ship's sudden deceleration — being whipped sideways by the rope chain. The female hunter's skimmer drifts further off-axis at the edge of frame.

Background sky: dense cross-hatching for golden hour, atmospheric haze in the deep distance.

Camera annotation: red pencil arrow showing camera position 3/4 behind Liu Hui, labeled "3/4 back / collapse opening." Red pencil emphasis marks on the buckling knee joints of the three strained mechanical legs.

Caption strip below panel: "SHOT 3 PART 1 / PANEL 3 — 3/4 BACK — SHIP BEGINS COLLAPSE / 28mm wide / handheld with subtle seismic vibration beginning / SFX: ship's leg-galloping rhythm breaking down, knee-joint metal groan, rope tension at maximum"

PANEL 4 (bottom-left cell) — SHOT 3 MIDDLE: BOW PLOUGHS INTO SAND:
Scene composition: same 3/4 back camera position as Panel 3, this is the next critical moment in the same continuous shot. Liu Hui's silhouette still occupies the foreground left area (back, shoulders, bronze spiral horns, cyan core glow) — the rope in his hands is going slack from peak tension, hanging in a curving slack line because the ship has lost its forward momentum. The protagonist ship dominates the middle and right area of frame: the bow has now ploughed forward into the sand at a tilted-forward angle of roughly 25 degrees, the front of the ship buried into the desert surface, three of the mechanical legs have fully buckled at the knee joints with armor segments splayed outward in awkward kneeling angles, the other 6 legs caught mid-collapse — some unable to compensate, some with feet not yet landed. The bow's impact point erupts in an enormous massive sand-and-dust plume that explodes upward and outward — the plume is drawn as dense cross-hatched cloud filling the bottom third of frame and partially obscuring the ship's lower body, with sand particles radiating outward in radial bursts as short pencil dot streaks. The two remaining sand skimmers are visible at the edges of the chaos — one tipped sideways, one being dragged into the dust cloud. The female hunter's skimmer is at the far right edge of frame, further off-axis now, having continued drifting during the collapse.

Background sky: dense cross-hatching for golden hour, the upper portion shows the massive dust plume rising into the sky above the impact point.

Camera annotation: red pencil emphasis marks at the bow-impact point with radial burst lines indicating the explosion. Red pencil arrow on the slack rope in Liu Hui's hands showing the tension release. Red pencil indicator showing camera seismic shake at peak.

Caption strip below panel: "SHOT 3 PART 2 / PANEL 4 — 3/4 BACK — BOW PLOUGH IMPACT / 28mm wide / handheld with seismic shake at peak / SFX: deafening THOOM of bow impact, massive sustained sand-explosion roar, mechanical leg-collapse buckling clanks, seismic ground shake"

PANEL 5 (bottom-middle cell) — SHOT 3 END: LIU HUI STANDS, SHIP HAS FALLEN:
Scene composition: same 3/4 back camera position as Panel 3 and 4, this is the end of the same continuous shot. Liu Hui's silhouette in the foreground left area now stands firm and still — back, shoulders, bronze spiral horns rising into upper frame, the cyan core has settled from peak power surge down to a calm baseline glow. The rope in his hands has gone fully slack and is partially trailing on the ground. Liu Hui's body is upright in the standing-firm pose, slightly elevated chin as he watches the result. The protagonist ship in the middle-to-right area of frame has come to rest in its final collapsed position: the bow deeply buried in the sand, the body tilted forward at roughly 25 degrees, three of the mechanical legs buckled in kneeling positions, the other 6 legs splayed in mid-collapse poses at awkward angles, the ship is now fully stationary. The massive sand-dust plume from the impact is still rising into the upper portion of frame but starting to settle and thin — particles slowly drifting downward at the edges of the plume. The two remaining sand skimmers have come to rest in their disrupted positions. The female hunter's skimmer is far off-axis at the edge of frame.

Background sky: dense cross-hatching for golden hour, the dust plume gradually fading into the upper atmosphere.

Camera annotation: red pencil arrow indicating camera has settled from seismic shake to stable handheld. Red pencil indicator on Liu Hui's settled posture, labeled "standing firm / watches the result."

Caption strip below panel: "SHOT 3 PART 3 / PANEL 5 — 3/4 BACK — FINAL STATE / 28mm wide / handheld settling to stable / SFX: dust plume slowly settling, Liu Hui's single heavy exhale, distant residual creaking of broken ship metal"

SHOOTING PLAN (bottom-right cell) — TOP-DOWN BIRD'S EYE OVERVIEW:
Drawn as a clean top-down schematic diagram on the aged paper. Layout uses the entire cell width effectively. Center-upper of the diagram: the protagonist ship drawn as a rectangular schematic silhouette with nine small leg-marks underneath, marked with a red pencil directional arrow showing its forward vector pointing toward the upper edge of the diagram. Between the ship and the foreground area below: two small triangular schematic outlines representing the two remaining sand skimmers, each connected by a thin line (rope) to one of the ship's leg-marks. Foreground-lower-left of the diagram: Liu Hui drawn as a small figure-outline with the rope extending from his hands as a thin straight line up to one of the ship's leg-marks (the rope passes alongside the two intermediate skimmers — clearly showing the rope is a single continuous line from Liu Hui to the ship). Far-right side of the diagram (off the main action chain): the female hunter's third skimmer drawn drifting off-axis at an angle, with a small dashed line showing its drift trajectory away from the chain — labeled "OFF-CHAIN / DRIFTING."

Three red pencil triangles around the action area marking the camera positions for each of the 3 shots: Shot 1 (extreme close-up) positioned very close to Liu Hui's hand, Shot 2 (side wide) positioned at side 90° to the action axis, Shot 3 (3/4 back covering Panels 3+4+5) positioned 3/4 behind Liu Hui — each red triangle is labeled with the shot number. A dashed red pencil straight line running across the diagram from Liu Hui through the ship — this is the 180-degree axis line. Labels in hand-lettered serif block letters: "180° AXIS LINE", "S1 / S2 / S3 CAMERA POSITIONS", "LIU HUI ANCHOR", "PROTAGONIST SHIP HEADING", "2 SKIMMERS IN CHAIN", "FEMALE HUNTER'S SKIMMER OFF-CHAIN".

Caption strip below the shooting plan cell: "SHOOTING PLAN — 180° AXIS PRESERVED / 3 hard-cut shot positions / S2 scale contrast is the dramatic anchor / S3 covers Panels 3+4+5 as one continuous take from one camera"

PRODUCTION NOTES at bottom edge of overall image (hand-lettered serif block letters along the bottom margin of the aged paper, outside the panel grid): "WANWU SHENG / EPISODE 1 / SEGMENT 9B — THE SHIP COMES DOWN / multi-shot hard cut sequence — NOT a continuous orbit one-take / continuity: prior segment 9A closes on Liu Hui having seized the rope and beginning to resist / this segment opens on the extreme close-up of the hand and rope tension moment / next segment 10 opens with the aftermath of the fallen ship"

Hard constraints:
- NO digital painting style, NO 3D render, NO color overlays except red colored pencil for arrows and annotations
- NO photographic elements, NO AI-style smooth gradients
- Strong golden-hour desert high-contrast lighting in all panels — dense cross-hatch shadows + selective bright untouched paper highlights, NOT evenly lit
- Each storyboard panel's inner frame strictly 16:9 aspect ratio
- Total image strictly 21:9 aspect ratio
- All hand-lettered text in confident serif block letters with slight unevenness — readable but not machine-printed
- Aged off-white paper texture throughout with subtle edge wear
- Maintain consistent fusion-form chieftain character appearance across all panels — same character with bronze mechanical armor, shoulder spiral horns visible in P2/P3/P4/P5, cyan chest core visible across all panels, partial face only in P1
- The scale contrast in PANEL 2 — Liu Hui's height vs ship's height at approximately 1:4 to 1:5 — must be visually unambiguous and is the dramatic anchor of the entire storyboard
- Panels 3, 4, 5 must show clearly that the camera position is unchanged (same 3/4 back angle) — they are three temporal moments within the same continuous Shot 3, not three different camera setups


### 第47条｜Layout: pure clean off-white backgroun

A professional 13-color palette design sheet for film production, in the style of a cinematographer's color script reference card. Image aspect ratio strictly 16:9 horizontal landscape format.

Layout: pure clean off-white background. Centered at top: large bold black sans-serif title "WANWU SHENG EP1 SEG10C - BIRD-DRAGON HUNTS CRAB BALL ON DECK - 13 COLOR PALETTE" in clean industrial typography.

Below the title: 13 vertical color swatch bars arranged in a single horizontal row, evenly spaced, each swatch is a tall vertical rectangle. Each color swatch is a flat solid color rectangle with crisp clean edges, no gradient, no texture inside the swatch itself. All 13 swatches are the same size and aligned on the same baseline.

Below each color swatch, hand-set typographic labels in two lines:
- Line 1: color name in clean bold sans-serif uppercase, two short lines if needed
- Line 2: HEX code in monospace sans-serif

The 13 colors in left-to-right order with their exact HEX values and labels:

1. SUNSET SKY ORANGE / #C8581F (visible sunset sky beyond deck edges)
2. WORN DECK WOOD BROWN / #6B4830 (weathered deck planks main wood color where the crab ball rolls)
3. BRONZE OLIVE MECH / #7A6845 (Bird-Dragon body armor + Crab outer shell + Bronze Ship Wheel shared mech color)
4. ANCIENT DARK GOLD / #846035 (ancient gold etched glyph plates on bird-dragon armor and crab symbol-engraved facets)
5. LOST-TECH CYAN GLOW / #4FC3D9 (Bird-Dragon cyan electric arc eye + Crab joint seam glow)
6. GOLDEN HOUR DIRECT LIGHT / #F0B670 (warm golden direct sunlight tint on lit surfaces)
7. COOL SHADOW CYAN-BLUE / #4A5870 (cool blue-tinted shadows on deck and under bird-dragon's wings)
8. WING FEATHER OCHRE CRIMSON / #6B4A2E (Bird-Dragon wing feather main color, ochre brown + red + dark gold blend)
9. WING FEATHER TIP DARK BROWN / #2E1F15 (Bird-Dragon wing feather tip darker brown + feather shadow recesses)
10. DUST PARTICLE HAZE / #C0B299 (dust kicked up by crab ball rolling + lingering deck dust + atmospheric haze)
11. POLISHED BRONZE BEAK HIGHLIGHT / #8E6F3D (Bird-Dragon mechanical beak polished bronze highlight on lit edge)
12. BEAK SHADOW BRONZE / #3A2A1F (Bird-Dragon beak shadow side dark bronze recess)
13. PITCH LIGHTLESS BLACK / #1A1610 (deep shadow anchor for crab ball silhouette and ship cabin deep shadow recess)

Each color swatch must render the exact HEX value with full color fidelity — flat solid color with no shading, gradient, or texture overlay. The label text below must be perfectly readable.

Color #6 GOLDEN HOUR DIRECT LIGHT is a warm peachy gold #F0B670 representing the actual color cast of golden hour sunlight on illuminated surfaces.
Color #7 COOL SHADOW CYAN-BLUE #4A5870 is a desaturated muted blue-grey representing the actual color tint of shadows during golden hour.
Color #10 DUST PARTICLE HAZE #C0B299 is a warm pale dust gray representing atmospheric haze and rolling dust particles in the desert deck environment.

The overall composition is a clean professional color reference sheet, similar to color scripts used in film production for cinematographers and colorists.

Hard constraints:
- All 13 color swatches must be exact HEX-accurate flat solid colors
- Strictly 16:9 horizontal landscape aspect ratio
- White or off-white pure background, NO illustrations, NO photographic elements, NO sample scenes
- Clean typographic labels in sans-serif font, fully readable
- Color swatches arranged in a single horizontal row with even spacing
- Each swatch is tall vertical rectangle with crisp clean edges
- No watermarks, no extra decorative elements
- The title at top is the largest text element
- The overall design feels professional, industrial, production-grade, like an Adobe Color or Pantone reference card crossed with a film production color script

【用途】4-15秒环绕镜头视频的首帧关键帧锚定+11秒视频起手姿态+24mm广角桶形畸变作为表现手法+Mad Max低机位追拍构图

Shot on 35mm Kodak Vision3 500T film stock + analog film grain visible + practical light only + Christopher Doyle cinematography + Wong Kar-wai painterly desaturation + photochemical film texture + slight halation on highlights + organic film noise + heavy atmospheric haze + dust particles in light beams + tactile real-world materials + lived-in worn surfaces + film print look not digital cinema clean + photorealistic film still aesthetic + real physical scene captured through a camera lens + not a 3D render. 【参考锁定】

@图3 作为整张图色卡锚定+所有色彩严格按色卡13色执行+不可偏离色板。

【镜头与机位】
Camera: 24mm wide-angle prime lens + intentional barrel distortion as artistic expression + dramatic forced perspective + Mad Max Fury Road style low-angle ground-level chase shot + the wide-angle barrel distortion is desired and accepted as a stylistic choice not a flaw.

Camera position: extremely low to the ground approximately 30cm above desert sand surface + camera body almost touching the ground + lens tilted slightly upward at 5-10 degrees from horizontal + camera positioned directly behind and slightly to the right of the running mechanical Saiga + the camera is essentially being dragged along the desert floor as the Saiga charges away from camera at high speed.

Frame: 16:9 horizontal aspect ratio cinematic single frame still.

【构图】
The frame is dominated by extreme forced perspective. In the bottom-left quadrant of the frame, the 
@图4 mechanical Saiga occupies approximately one-third of the lower-left area, captured in mid-charge mid-stride. Due to the 24mm wide-angle low-angle perspective, the Saiga's rear hooves closest to the camera appear massive and distorted, occupying the immediate foreground bottom of the frame - one rear mechanical hoof is frozen mid-impact with the desert ground, kicking up a dramatic plume of #C49B5C ochre golden sand spraying outward toward the camera lens, the hoof itself appears 3-4 times larger than it would in a normal lens due to forced perspective wide-angle distortion.

The Saiga's body extends diagonally from bottom-left foreground toward the middle of the frame, body angled in profile/3-4 view facing screen-right (away from camera into the deeper background), the bronze armor plates of #7A6845 mecha bronze olive catching the dusk light along the back ridge, the twin spire horns of #846035 ancient gold pointing skyward and slightly distorted by the wide-angle perspective at the upper-left edge, the single forehead glowing eye of #4FC3D9 cyan electric arc visible in profile, ancient symbol engravings catching shadow.

Mounted on the Saiga's back, captured from the rear-quarter low-angle view, sits 
@图1 Liu Hui the Sand Hunter chieftain. Liu Hui's body is twisted slightly, his upper torso visible above the Saiga's bronze armor saddle area, riding low and aggressive in a hunter's posture leaning forward into the charge. His scarred face is partially turned showing his profile - the eye-patched left side facing camera with #4A2F1F dark brown leather patch and #5A2A1F dark blood-red scar visible. His spiky black hair flies wildly in the wind. His #6B2419 deep crimson and #B8A082 cream tribal scarves trail violently behind him in the slipstream. His mechanical prosthetic right arm of #2D2A2A dark gunmetal gray and #7A6845 bronze grips the Saiga's reins or saddle structure. His bare tattooed torso shows tribal tattoos in #5A2A1F dark blood-red ink. The large curved blade strapped diagonally across his back is visible.

In the upper-right quadrant of the frame, in the deep background far away, visible at much smaller scale due to the distance: @图6 the protagonist's massive mechanical desert ship is seen running away on its 9 mechanical insect legs, bronze hull and cyan electric arcs glowing, occupying about 1/8 of the frame area in the upper-right distance. Flanking the ship to its left and right at slightly closer middle-distance, two 
@图5 sand-skiffs are pursuing the ship, smaller than Liu Hui's foreground Saiga but larger than the distant ship, kicking up plumes of dust trailing behind them.

The middle and lower-right portion of the frame: vast desert ground of #8E5A2A earth orange stretching toward the distant horizon, #C49B5C ochre golden sand dunes rolling in the mid-distance, golden hour lighting bathing everything in warm amber tones.

The horizon line sits roughly at the middle-upper third of the frame, above which the #C8581F burnt orange dusk sky dominates with #F5C870 golden sun glow at the upper-right edge from where the dusk sun is setting.

【光线】
Golden hour late afternoon dusk lighting. The sun is positioned at the upper-right of the frame at low angle approximately 10-15 degrees above the horizon, casting long horizontal shadows leftward across the desert. The Saiga and Liu Hui in the foreground are partially backlit by the dusk sun, creating dramatic rim-light edges of #F5C870 golden glow along the Saiga's bronze back ridge and Liu Hui's hair and shoulder edges. Their forward-facing surfaces (toward camera) fall into deeper shadow tones of #1A1610 deep black. Atmospheric haze of #D9A55C amber dust suspended in the air, golden god-rays cutting through the airborne dust. Dust particles kicked up by the Saiga's hooves catch the dusk light dramatically.

【空间关系】
The Saiga is the closest object to camera, in foreground bottom-left.
Liu Hui is mounted on the Saiga, slightly further back in the same foreground volume.
The two sand-skiffs are in middle-distance, upper-middle area of frame.
The protagonist's ship is the furthest object, in deep background upper-right.
This establishes the chase hierarchy: Liu Hui on Saiga is rapidly catching up from behind, with two sand-skiffs ahead of him also pursuing the ship which is the prey running away into the distance.

The Saiga is moving in a direction that will lead it past the sand-skiffs and toward the protagonist's ship - established through the angle of charge and the relative spatial positions.

【色彩】
Strict adherence to 
@图3 color palette: #1A1610 deep shadow black, #7A6845 mecha bronze olive for Saiga and Liu Hui's prosthetic, #846035 ancient gold for Saiga horns and metal accents, #4FC3D9 cyan electric arc for Saiga's eye and any lost-tech glow, #F5C870 golden sun rim-light, #C8581F burnt orange dusk sky, #6B2419 deep crimson Sand Hunter tribal red for Liu Hui's scarves, #B8A082 cream cloth for Liu Hui's tribal scarf cream wrap, #4A2F1F eye patch dark brown leather, #5A2A1F dark blood-red scar and tattoo color, #2D2A2A tattoo and prosthetic dark gunmetal gray, #5A4830 ancient symbol shadow recess, #8E5A2A desert earth orange ground, #C49B5C ochre golden dune highlights and kicked-up sand.

【风格质感】
Photorealistic film still captured with real 35mm motion picture camera + Kodak Vision3 500T 500-speed tungsten-balanced color negative film stock + analog film grain texture visible at all scales + photochemical halation around bright highlights + slight subtle anamorphic lens flare from the dusk sun + organic film noise + heavy desert atmospheric haze + dust particles suspended in golden light beams + tactile weathered real-world surface materials on every object + lived-in battle-worn appearance on the Saiga's bronze armor and Liu Hui's clothing.

【关键约束】
镜头铁律: 24mm wide-angle prime lens with INTENTIONAL barrel distortion + dramatic forced perspective + foreground objects exaggerated in size due to wide-angle proximity effect + Mad Max Fury Road low-angle ground-level chase cinematography style + the camera is positioned approximately 30cm above desert ground level looking up at slight angle.

构图铁律: Saiga and Liu Hui occupy bottom-left third of frame foreground + protagonist ship occupies upper-right deep background + two sand-skiffs occupy upper-middle distance between them + horizon line sits at middle-upper third of frame + Saiga's rear hoof is dramatically enlarged in immediate foreground bottom due to wide-angle proximity.

风格铁律: 35mm film stock photography texture+analog film grain+photochemical look+NOT 3D rendered+NOT CGI+NOT video game graphics+NOT Unreal Engine render+NOT Blender render+NOT clean digital surface+NOT plastic look+NOT smooth artificial texture+all surfaces must show real physical wear and weathering+film print quality not digital cinema clean.

主体身份铁律: Saiga must match 
@图4 reference exactly + four mechanical legs not more not less + twin spire horns + single forehead cyan glowing eye + bronze armor plate body + ancient symbol engravings. Liu Hui must match 
@图1 reference exactly + male middle-aged + one eye covered by leather patch on left side + facial scar + spiky black hair + mechanical right arm + bare tattooed torso + tribal scarves + curved blade strapped to back.

NOT rectilinear projection (this frame embraces wide-angle distortion as expression) + NOT distortion-free + NOT 50mm or longer focal length + NOT centered framing + NOT static peaceful scene+NOT clear blue sky+NOT modern vehicles+NOT modern weapons+NOT cyberpunk neon+NOT clean digital surface+NOT cartoon+NOT chibi+NOT illustrated+NOT painterly+NOT concept art+NOT digital painting+NOT matte painting+NOT typical AI generated illustration aesthetic+无字幕水印边框装饰花纹。
