### 第91条｜— Reference: the male character. Prese

REFERENCE IMAGE:
@图1 — Reference: the male character. Preserve his identity, full body design, the black LED-panel face with two glowing teal-green light points serving as eyes, his messy black human hair falling over the panel, his mechanical segmented neck, his teal-green crystal earrings, his costume (burgundy/dark-red sleeveless long vest with leather and metal armor accents, cream-white billowing pirate sleeves, dark burgundy fitted pants, brown leather over-the-knee boots, brown leather messenger satchel, brass ring buckle on the chest, dark leather strap diagonally across torso), all material qualities, color palette, and overall aesthetic exactly from this reference. He is a young man, lean build, late teens to early twenties.

INSTRUCTION:
Generate a 16:9 aspect ratio editorial magazine spread for a male character introduction page, in the visual language of contemporary high-fashion editorial design (i-D Magazine, Dazed, AnOther Magazine reference). This is the second card in a series — the first card featured a female character in the same editorial style. This card must feel like a companion piece to the first card while introducing this male character as a separate identity.

COMPOSITION (16:9 landscape):

The young man from the Reference image is positioned in the center-LEFT portion of the frame, occupying approximately 30-35 percent of the canvas width horizontally and 55-65 percent of the canvas height vertically. He is rendered in a kneeling-folded pose described in detail below, with the figure occupying the lower-to-middle portion of the frame, leaving more empty beige background above him than in a standing composition.

THE POSE (CRITICAL — a stylized, unreadable, ambiguous kneeling-folded pose):

His body is folded into a kneeling posture that reads as ambiguous — neither clearly active nor clearly defeated, neither clearly resting nor clearly broken. Specifically:

LOWER BODY: His right leg is bent under him in a full kneel with the knee on the ground, his weight settling onto that knee. His left leg is bent upward in front of him with the foot planted flat on the ground and the knee raised, forming a propped-up bent-knee position. The two legs together create an asymmetric folded silhouette — one knee down, one knee up.

TORSO: His upper body LEANS FORWARD over his raised left knee, the torso hunched, chest tilted down. The forward-leaning torso compresses against his thighs slightly, the burgundy vest and cream sleeves bunching with the fold. His posture has the quality of someone who has paused mid-action, or someone who has been gently set down by an unseen force — it is not a triumphant pose, not a defeated collapse, but something in between that cannot be easily categorized.

ARMS AND HANDS: Both arms hang loosely from his shoulders, draping down on either side of his raised left knee. His wrists are slack and the hands hang limp, fingers slightly curled in a "just stopped moving" position — as if mid-gesture and arrested. No tension in the hands, no purpose in the gesture. The brown leather satchel hangs heavily from its strap, dragged forward by the lean of his torso.

HEAD AND FACE PANEL: His black LED face panel is angled DOWN toward the camera-forward direction but partially OBSCURED by his raised left knee, so only the lower half of the panel and the two teal-green eye lights are visible above the knee — the upper portion of the panel is cut off from view by his own leg. His messy black hair cascades fully downward, covering the upper edges of the panel and hanging over his shoulders. The mechanical segmented neck is partially compressed by the forward-lean, with one or two of the metal segments visibly angled slightly out of alignment — as if the folding posture has slightly distressed the mechanical structure. The teal-green crystal earrings dangle straight down from the bent-forward head.

OVERALL: The pose is sculptural and ambiguous. It is NOT a JoJo joint-dislocation pose, NOT a JoJo Araki extreme stance. It is something quieter and more disturbing — a pose where you cannot tell if he sat down voluntarily or was placed there by someone else, whether he is contemplating or shutting down, whether he is alive or in standby mode. The pose is the visual equivalent of a question mark.

THE CORE TENSION OF THE POSE:
The body posture would, on a human character, read as "exhausted resting" or "quiet contemplation" — but because his face is an unreadable black LED panel with no expression, the meaning of the pose is completely undefined. The viewer cannot read whether he is alive or off, awake or asleep, present or absent. His own knee partially obscures his face, removing even the two eye-lights from full view. The hands are limp, the body is folded, the face is hidden — every signal the viewer would use to read a character has been deliberately muted. The pose is itself a question mark.

The dominant background filling most of the canvas is the SAME foggy warm beige (#C9B89A to #B8A88A range) used in the companion character card — to maintain series consistency. NOT bright cream, NOT yellow, NOT brown — mid-tone beige with slight gray undertone, subtle paper-grain texture, slightly weathered. This mid-tone beige must be calibrated so the character's dark burgundy vest reads darker against it AND his cream-white shirt reads brighter against it — clean figure-ground separation.
